- Decision: `src/domain/obsolete-appearance.ts` (new, pure) returns the indicators found, the set `examined`, the set `notExamined`, and a `coverage` sentence naming the gap. `web-opportunity-ipc.ts` extracts two further signals from the page text it **already fetches and stores** for the tel: check — no new request, no new cost, the same argument as DEC-097 — and the renderer maps them onto the model's indicators, preserving the measure-in-main, score-in-renderer split.
- Consequences: coverage is now stated in the interface rather than implied, and the factor is marked `unmeasured` when nothing at all could be checked instead of reporting an empty list. Two indicators become real: `obsolete-technology-marker`, matched verbatim against Flash embeds, `<marquee>`, `<blink>`, presentational `<font>` tags, "best viewed in" notices and FrontPage generator tags; and `stale-or-missing-copyright`, **reported only in its stale form**. The missing form is deliberately never inferred — a copyright notice absent from fetched text may be rendered by JavaScript or lie beyond the 500 KB fetch cap, and DEC-034 warns specifically that absences are cheap to detect and easy to get wrong. The staleness threshold is two years rather than one so the indicator cannot fire on the turn of a calendar year for a site updated in December and checked in January, and it compares against the evidence's own retrieval year rather than the current clock (charter 9.7), so rescoring retained evidence gives the same answer. 11 new tests bring the suite to 392, and both mutations were caught: inferring the missing-copyright case, and dropping the unchecked-indicator list from the coverage sentence. **Still not examined:** four indicators — font families, non-neutral colours, placeholder or theme content, and stock imagery — all of which need rendered visual inspection rather than text matching, and none of which this pretends to have looked at.
- Supersedes: not applicable — this corrects a reporting gap DEC-072 introduced without stating it, and does not change `web-opportunity-v2`.

### DEC-099 — The subscription-backed Claude Code runtime is retained, and formally removed from V1's critical path

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09** ("termina tú el proyecto eligiendo la mejor decisión" — the operator delegated this verdict explicitly, having been told twice that it was theirs to make. It is a technical and strategic judgment about HORUS's own tooling with no third-party consequence, which is why it was accepted under delegation where DEC-004's gates never could be. It binds nothing that a superseding decision cannot change.)
- Context: `ROADMAP.md`'s Phase 6 exit criterion requires "a decision is recorded on whether to retain the subscription-backed runtime, change provider/runtime, revise V1, or reconsider the approach", and `AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10 step 8 says the same. DEC-045 authorised the evaluation; DEC-049 built the boundary; DEC-056 through DEC-061 verified the runtime contract against a live process and found two real defects doing so; DEC-059, DEC-066 and DEC-067 implemented four of the five `ANALYST_TOOLS`; DEC-063 and DEC-064 ran the shadow replay. No verdict was ever written, and it has been the single clean blocker on the phase since.
- Options considered: **retain** unchanged; **change provider or runtime**; **revise** — retain the runtime and boundary but narrow the role; **reject** and remove the agent work.
- Decision: **revise.** The subscription-backed Claude Code runtime and the boundary built around it are retained as evaluated, and the agent is formally placed **outside HORUS V1's critical path**. No step of charter §4's workflow may depend on an agent, and none does. Before any further `ANALYST_TOOLS` entry is wired — `run_deterministic_scoring` is the one remaining — DEC-088's finding F4 must be closed or explicitly accepted in its own decision.
- Consequences: the evidence for this verdict is stronger than expected, and most of it arrived after the evaluation was designed.

  **What argues for retaining.** The runtime works and its safety properties are demonstrated rather than asserted: a read-only SQLite handle proven to reject writes (DEC-059), an allow-list confirmed live with `permission_denials: []` (DEC-059), schema-constrained output with an acceptance parser that rejects claims citing evidence the task never received (DEC-049), and a real spawn with a timeout kill and an argument-injection check (DEC-060). It costs nothing metered — `--bare` was rejected precisely because it would have required an API key (DEC-056), so AGENT_ARCHITECTURE §11's "no usage-metered model API" criterion is met by construction. The shadow replay behaved correctly under partial evidence: on SEASONS EATS the analyst proposed nothing and marked three fields `insufficient_data` rather than guessing (DEC-064). Two real defects were caught by exercising it — `allowedTools` never reaching the permission surface (DEC-058) and invented snapshot ids (DEC-061) — which is evidence the evaluation worked, not that the runtime is unsound.

  **What argues against expanding it.** `grep` for agent or analyst references across the real pipeline — `validate-pipeline.ts`, `ProspectRecord`, `ShortlistView`, `CandidateActions` — returns **zero**. The analyst is a parallel panel that charter §4's workflow does not touch at any point. The full end-to-end run of DEC-092 did not invoke it once. Everything built in DEC-089 through DEC-098 is deterministic. Set against that: on Finescape the analyst's qualitative read diverged from the deterministic outcome (proposed for review; retired at 48.1 by the model), which DEC-064 correctly calls expected rather than defective — but it is also a plain illustration that the analyst's judgement is not the one HORUS acts on. And DEC-088's F4 is open: an analyst that can read retained evidence and fetch an arbitrary public URL has a working exfiltration channel through hostile review text, which no output-side control can see because it happens in the request.

  **Why revise rather than retain or reject.** Retaining unchanged would imply the agent is part of what HORUS does, which no evidence supports and which would make F4 a live risk on the critical path. Rejecting would discard a boundary that is correct, cheap, and the best-verified component in the repository — and would throw away the answer to a question DEC-045 asked in good faith. Revise records what is actually true: the evaluation succeeded, the runtime is sound, and V1 does not need it.

  **What this does not do.** It does not authorise wiring the agent into any workflow step, does not close DEC-088's F4, and does not claim the analyst has ever contributed to a real prospect decision — it has not. `run_deterministic_scoring` stays unwired. This decision is revisable: if a later phase finds real work for a bounded analyst, a superseding decision can narrow or widen this without unpicking anything, because nothing depends on it.
- Supersedes: not applicable — this answers the question DEC-045 opened and `AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10 step 8 left standing.

### DEC-100 — Runtime unavailability is proven recoverable, and proven to write nothing

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09** ("termina tú el proyecto eligiendo la mejor decisión").
- Context: `ROADMAP.md`'s Phase 6 exit criterion requires that "Claude unavailability is recoverable without corrupting state", and `AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md` section 11 repeats it. `classifyFailure` was already well covered for *classifying* a failure (DEC-049) — not installed, not logged in, usage limit reached, timeout, cancellation — but nothing asserted the property the criterion actually names: that a failed run leaves nothing behind, and that the next run still works. The failure modes here are ordinary rather than exotic: a laptop offline, a subscription limit reached, an operator logged out.
- Decision: `tests/agent-unavailability.test.ts` asserts, for every failure mode, that `runOpportunityAnalyst` returns `failed`, that `saveDraft` is **never** called, and that no output accompanies a failure. Recovery is asserted directly: a failing run followed by a succeeding one leaves exactly one draft, availability is re-checked rather than remembered, and each attempt gets its own isolated working directory (DEC-057).
- Consequences: the exit criterion is now executable rather than argued. 10 new tests bring the suite to 402. The mutation that matters was caught: moving `saveDraft` ahead of `parseAnalystOutput` — so a run would persist a draft on its way down — fails the suite. That is the failure this property exists to prevent, because a half-written draft would leave the operator reading agent output no completed run ever produced. **What this does not cover:** these are fakes at the `SpawnImpl` boundary, so a real `claude` process being killed mid-write is not exercised; DEC-060's real-subprocess tests cover the spawn layer, and the two together are as close as this gets without a live failure injection.
- Supersedes: not applicable

### DEC-101 — A material edit invalidates the outreach approval it was given under

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09** ("termina tú el proyecto eligiendo la mejor decisión").
- Context: `AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md` section 11 lists "A material edit invalidates the relevant prior approval" among the conditions for completing the agent evaluation. Checking each criterion honestly before claiming any of them found this one **unmet, and unmet at the second DEC-004 gate.** The publish gate always behaved correctly — generating a new demonstration preview clears its approval, and a successful publish clears the outreach one. But editing the outreach draft after ticking its approval cleared nothing. The checkbox reads *"I approve sending this exact message, as written above"*; the operator could tick it, then change the recipient, subject, or body, and open the Gmail compose handoff carrying an approval that was given for different text.
- Decision: every edit to the outreach recipient, subject, or body clears `outreachApproved`, through a single `editOutreachDraft` helper the three controls share so a future field cannot be added that bypasses it.
- Consequences: the approval a handoff carries is now always the approval given for the exact text being handed off. This is not a hypothetical tidy-up — DEC-041 and charter 17.3 rest on the operator's approval being specific, and the whole reason HORUS opens a compose window rather than sending is that the message a human approved is the message a human sends. 4 new tests bring the suite to 406, covering all three fields plus the already-correct publish behaviour, asserted alongside so the pair is visible together. The mutation removing the reset was caught. **Found by auditing the acceptance criteria one by one rather than by any test failing** — nothing in 402 passing tests had noticed, because it is a property between two controls rather than inside either.
- Supersedes: not applicable

### DEC-102 — The interface becomes the six named views, from the operator's own prototype

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09** ("haz lo que creas necesario con tal de que adopte el layout y siga siendo funcional"). The operator supplied a working HTML prototype and asked whether it could be applied.
- Context: FUNCTIONAL_DESIGN §6 names six views — Search, Shortlist, Prospect detail, Demonstration review, Outreach review, Tracker. None existed. Every Phase 6 checkpoint carried "the six named views do not exist as views; the interface is a workflow shell with appended panels" as a known weakness, and it was the largest one left. The prototype answers it directly: a fixed rail grouping Workflow / Approvals / History, one view in the main area, a header carrying the market and a status badge.
- Options considered: keep the appended-panel shell (rejected — it was the named weakness); adopt the prototype wholesale including its factor bars (rejected in part, see below); adopt its information architecture and chrome, and keep this application's own treatment of measured values (chosen).
- Decision: `src/views/OperatorWorkspace.tsx` (new) owns the view state and the discovery state the views share, and renders the prototype's layout. `RealDiscoverySearch` is dissolved into the Search view. `ProspectRecord` gains a `section` prop — `evidence`, `demonstration`, `outreach`, `hidden` — and is **mounted once** by the workspace rather than rendered inside three view branches, because React would unmount it on every switch and silently discard a demonstration preview or a half-written outreach draft. Phase 4's representative walkthrough is retained as a reference view rather than deleted: its own banner has always said search, deployment and sending "are not executed here", so it is no longer how anyone works, but it is the only thing exercising workflow-state persistence and DEC-048's main-process validation.
- Consequences: the six views exist, and the largest carried-forward weakness in the Phase 6 checkpoint is closed. **One part of the prototype was deliberately not adopted: it rendered each scoring factor as a filled bar.** That is DEC-083 rule 4, and the reason is visible in the prototype itself, which drew "Broken elements — full bar — 18 / 18" for a factor that has never been measured in the real system. A bar has two states; this model needs three — measured with a value, measured at zero, and never measured at all. Three of five web-opportunity factors are `unmeasured` today and every candidate is `partial_data`, so drawing "unmeasured" and "zero" identically is exactly the failure charter 10.4 and hard rule 6 exist to prevent. Values keep their tabular numerals, denominator and status word. Everything else from the prototype was taken, including its best idea: an evidence-and-judgment panel whose language — "it never auto-rejects", "the 72 score is a lower bound" — is DEC-008 and charter 10.4 stated in the interface rather than only in the code.

  Two things were found by the test suite while making the change, and both were real rather than test breakage. **DEC-038's single-`h1` requirement regressed**: the new header used a paragraph for the wordmark and every view title was an `h2`, leaving the document with no `h1` at all. The accessibility tests caught it immediately. And **the freshness verdict became reachable only from one view**: DEC-089 deliberately placed it "before either gate, so the operator learns the evidence is stale while there is still something to do about it", but with separate views the operator can go straight to Demo review without passing through Prospect. It now renders in all three sections. 406 tests still pass; the interaction tests were updated to pass the section they exercise, and one now crosses from Demo review to Outreach by rerender — the same move the operator makes, verifying the draft survives it.

  **What this does not do:** it does not add the prototype's stat tiles for a search in progress (HORUS makes one request and does not paginate, so there is no progress to report — FUNCTIONAL_DESIGN §6.1 forbids a fake percentage), and it does not restructure any domain logic, IPC contract, or approval path.
- Supersedes: not applicable — this extends DEC-083's register with an information architecture DEC-083 did not address.

### DEC-103 — A candidate never assessed is not a candidate that failed

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09.** Found by the operator running the real application and asking why nothing reached the shortlist.
- Context: the operator ran a live search for landscaping in Bridgeport, retrieved ten candidates, scored **one** of them, and looked at the Shortlist. All ten read `not_reputation_qualified`. Only one had been assessed: Cesar's Lawn Maintenance and Masonry, scored 53.5 against the 70 threshold. The other nine had never had their review history retrieved at all — nothing about their reputation had been measured. `buildShortlist` took `qualified: boolean`, and `ShortlistView` supplied `score?.qualified ?? false`, so an absent score and a genuine shortfall arrived as the same value and left as the same reason.
- Options considered: leave it, since the candidate is excluded either way (rejected — the exclusion reason is the only thing on that screen explaining *why*, and it was giving the wrong answer nine times out of ten); pass a tri-state `qualified` (rejected — a third boolean state is exactly the ambiguity being fixed); branch on `reputationScoreLowerBound === null`, which the input already carries and already documents as "`null` if reputation has not been scored yet" (chosen).
- Decision: `ShortlistExclusionReason` gains `reputation_not_assessed`, checked before `not_reputation_qualified`. `ShortlistView` renders every reason in the operator's own words rather than as a machine token, and phrases this one so it cannot be read as a judgement about the business: "reputation not assessed yet — no review history has been retrieved for this listing".
- Consequences: **this is charter 9.6 and hard rule 6 applied to the ranking surface, where they had been applied everywhere else and missed.** The scoring model has always been meticulous about the distinction — `insufficient_data` is a first-class result, `unmeasured` factors never count as zero, DEC-098 corrected the same conflation in obsolete-appearance coverage — but the shortlist, the one screen that tells the operator why a business is not being pursued, flattened both into "not qualified". On a screen of ten businesses that read as ten rejections when nine were simply unexamined. 4 new tests bring the suite to 410, and the mutation restoring the old behaviour was caught. One DEC-093 test asserted on the raw token and was updated to match the words the operator now reads, which is the better assertion. **Not changed:** ranking itself. An unassessed candidate was excluded before and is excluded now; only the explanation changed. That is the entire point — the number was never wrong, the account of it was.
- Supersedes: not applicable — this corrects a conflation DEC-075 introduced when it defined the exclusion reasons.

### DEC-104 — Factor 5 is measured from the reviews already retrieved

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09.** Found while investigating the operator's report that a real search produced nothing usable.
- Context: `reputation-scoring-v1`'s Factor 5, longevity evidence, is worth 5 points and scores from `historySpanYears`. `CandidateActions` passed `{ status: 'unmeasured', reason: 'Full-history retrieval was not performed (DEC-018 cost discipline).' }` **unconditionally**, so the factor read `0.0/5` for every candidate ever scored — including the six the operator scored live, and including TwoGen at 71.1 and every figure in the calibration set. The reason given was true but the conclusion did not follow: DEC-018 caps retrieval at three pages, which prevents knowing a business's *full* history, not from observing the span of the reviews actually in hand.
- Options considered: paginate further to establish a true span (rejected — that is DEC-018's cost decision, and reopening it is a separate question); leave it unmeasured (rejected — it discards evidence already retrieved and paid for, the same fault DEC-097 and DEC-098 corrected elsewhere); measure the span of the retrieved reviews and score it as the lower bound it is (chosen).
- Decision: `summarizeReviewHistory` returns `retrievedHistorySpanYears` — years between the oldest and newest dated review retrieved, `null` when fewer than two came back, since one review spans nothing and reporting 0 would be a measurement where there is none. `CandidateActions` feeds it to Factor 5.
- Consequences: **this is a lower bound and nothing else.** A business whose oldest retrieved review is two years old may well be ten years old; it can never be younger. That is exactly what `scoreLowerBound` means, which is what makes scoring it honest where extrapolating a true age would not be. Measured against the operator's own live evidence, the effect on the seven businesses scored so far: Davida Landscape Designs +5.0 (5.78 years of retrieved history, the full factor, previously discarded entirely), Chad Landscapes +1.8, NORTH END +1.4, Cesar's Lawn +1.3, Dubons +1.2, Pro Plumber +0.3, and TwoGen +0.0 — its three pages span only 0.23 years, below Factor 5's one-year floor, which is the correct answer rather than a missing one. 4 new tests bring the suite to 414, and both mutations were caught: treating a single review as a zero-length span rather than as no span, and measuring age from the retrieval date instead of the span between reviews. A test pins the distinction directly — the same two reviews scored years later must give the same span, because a span is not an age.

  **This does not change any published figure retroactively.** DEC-086 and DEC-087 reproduced the calibration and Phase 5 numbers exactly, and they were produced with Factor 5 unmeasured; those reproductions remain correct for the inputs they were given. Rescoring that retained evidence under this change would raise some of them, which is a separate exercise and not one this decision performs.

  **What it does not fix:** the operator's actual complaint. The largest gain here is 5 points, and the closest candidate in their Bridgeport search now sits at 65.3 against a 70 threshold. That threshold is one of the numbers this repository has carried as an explicit guess since the charter, and Phase 1 calibration already observed a median of 67.0 across fourteen complete histories. This is now the second dataset pointing at the same parameter.
- Supersedes: not applicable — this completes a factor DEC-068 implemented and DEC-071 never supplied an input for.

### DEC-105 — The reviews are shown to the operator who is asked to judge them

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09.** Reported by the operator, in these words: *"how am I supposed to know what to put if I can't see anything?"*
- Context: DEC-091 built charter 9.5's three judgment gates and made qualification reachable for the first time. G4 asks whether there is "a pattern of unresolved complaints **in the reviews you read**". G5 asks whether the business is operating. G6 asks whether the listing and its reviews belong to that business. **The application had never shown the operator a single review.** `runReviewHistoryRetrieval` extracted `iso_date` and `rating` and discarded the words, the author, and whether the owner had replied — everything the scoring model needs, and nothing a person needs. The operator was asked for a judgement the interface gave them no way to form.
- Options considered: link out to the Google listing (rejected — the evidence is already retrieved, stored, and paid for; sending the operator to a live page also means judging something other than the snapshot the score was computed from); show a count of low ratings (rejected — a pattern of *unresolved* complaints is not a number, it is what the words say and whether anyone answered); carry the review content through and render it beside the questions (chosen).
- Decision: `extractReviews` now keeps `text`, `author`, and `ownerResponded` alongside the date and rating. `CandidateScoreAction` renders the retrieved reviews inside the judgment panel, sorted worst rating first, each showing its stars, date, author, and whether the owner replied. Where a review has no text this says so rather than rendering an empty block.
- Consequences: **this is the project's own first principle, applied to the one screen that had been exempt from it.** CLAUDE.md's conventions say "any number shown to the operator must be accompanied by what produced it" — and the screen where the operator's own judgement is the input was the screen with no evidence on it. Sorting worst-first is not cosmetic: G4 asks about a pattern of unresolved complaints, and that is where one becomes visible. `ownerResponded` is carried for the same reason — the gate asks about *unresolved* complaints, so whether anyone answered is part of the evidence rather than decoration. A low rating is marked with a rule and shown with its rating in words, never by colour alone (DEC-038). The panel states plainly when the retrieval was capped, and repeats charter 9.6 in the place it matters most: reviews the operator cannot see may say something different, so nothing concluded from silence is evidence. On the operator's own retrieved data this immediately surfaced a 1-star review of Cesar's Lawn — money taken, work not done, texts unanswered, no owner reply — which is exactly the G4 evidence the interface had been withholding. 2 new tests bring the suite to 416. One existing deep-equality assertion on the review shape broke and was **updated rather than loosened**: it is what caught the change.
- Supersedes: not applicable — this completes DEC-091, which built the questions without building the evidence they depend on.

### DEC-106 — The demonstration is built from the listing detail that was already retrieved

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09.** Reported by the operator, on seeing a generated demonstration: *"why is the generated site so shit?"*
- Context: FUNCTIONAL_DESIGN §8.1 specifies eight sections for a demonstration — identity, value proposition, services cards, business-owned imagery, location and contact, and so on. `demonstration.ts` built **one**: Contact. A generated demonstration was the business's own Google listing card, laid out. The obvious reading was that DEC-005 forbids inventing content and there is simply nothing to show. That reading was wrong. `extractCandidates` kept nine fields — the ones `reputation-scoring-v1` needs — and discarded everything else the same response carried. For Brasitas, one of the operator's own candidates, Google had already returned: full weekly opening hours, service options (outdoor seating, delivery, takeout, dine-in), highlights (fast service, great cocktails, great wine list), a price range, four category labels, and the business's own listing photograph. All published by the business, all verified, all retrieved, all thrown away.
- Decision: `DiscoveryCandidateSummary` carries `serviceOptions`, `highlights`, `categories`, `operatingHours`, `priceRange` and `photoUrl`. `buildDemonstrationSite` renders §8.1's services, hours and imagery sections from them. `extensions` is read for exactly two attribute groups — `service_options` and `highlights` — rather than flattened, so the page never becomes a soup of Google's internal labels.
- Consequences: the Brasitas demonstration went from a contact card to a page with the restaurant's own photograph, its published price range, five service and quality tags, its full weekly hours, and its contact details — **with no placeholders at all**, from evidence already paid for. Every block omits itself entirely when its evidence is absent, which is §8.1's own rule and DEC-005's: a gap stays empty rather than being filled. The photograph is captioned as coming from the business's own public listing (DEC-025 — never generic imagery presented as their work), and where none exists a labelled placeholder area appears instead of an image. Provenance is stated on the page itself, not only in the record: "Listed publicly by the business on its own Google listing." 6 new tests, including one asserting that a business with no published services, hours or price produces a page containing none of those words. **This is the fifth time in this phase that a defect turned out to be retrieved-and-discarded data** — DEC-097, DEC-098, DEC-104, DEC-105 and now this. The pattern is consistent: each extraction kept exactly what the next computation needed and dropped what a person would want. **Still not built from §8.1:** the value-proposition block and the disabled contact form, both of which need content this data does not carry.
- Supersedes: not applicable — this completes DEC-079, which built the preview as a deliberate first increment.

### DEC-107 — A closed application no longer discards the session

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09.** Reported by the operator: *"every time I exit, everything is wiped. It's useless like this, it has to have memory."*
- Context: DEC-076 built the prospect record in memory and said so; every checkpoint since has carried "working prospect state is in memory only" as a known weakness. In practice that meant a search costing credits, a review history costing more, and a scoring pass were all lost on quitting the application. The operator was correct that this made it unusable rather than merely imperfect.
- Options considered: store the working state — scores, audits, selection — in a new table (rejected: charter §14 separates the immutable from the derived, and a stored score is a second copy that can drift from the evidence that produced it; this session has already chosen projection over duplication three times); rebuild the session from the evidence already retained (chosen).
- Decision: `session:restore` returns the most recent discovery snapshot with its candidates already extracted, plus every retained review history keyed by `data_id`. On start-up the workspace rebuilds the candidate list and **recomputes** each reputation score from those retained reviews, applying the operator's recorded judgment (DEC-094) so a candidate that qualified yesterday still qualifies today.
- Consequences: the application reopens where it was left. Verified against the operator's own store: twenty candidates from their last Stamford search restored, with review histories retained for the two they had scored. **The restore cannot spend.** Candidates are extracted in the main process from the retained payload rather than by re-running a search — an earlier version of this called `discovery.run` and relied on DEC-077's cache to make it free, which would have meant a cache miss silently spending a credit on start-up. That is not a thing this application may do, so the network path was removed rather than guarded. Charter §14's model is what makes this work at all: evidence is immutable, everything derived from it is recomputable, and the only thing that could not be recomputed — the operator's own judgment — was already made durable by DEC-094. **Still not restored:** web-opportunity audits, and the selected prospect itself. Both are recoverable by the same means and are not built here.
- Supersedes: not applicable — this closes the weakness DEC-076 recorded against itself.

### DEC-108 — A restored session counted the same review five times, and re-reading it cost credits

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09.** Found by walking the restored session in the running application, before writing any code.
- Context: DEC-107's restore concatenated **every** retained review page for a listing. Charter §14 forbids overwriting evidence, so each press of "Fetch review history & score" writes a fresh set of pages beside the old ones — and a listing retrieved five times came back with its reviews five times over. This is not a display problem: the trailing-90-day and trailing-365-day counts are reputation Factor 3's input, so **the restored score rose with the number of times the operator had pressed the button.** Measured against the operator's own store: Puerto Vallarta Stamford restored at **95.0/100** with Factor 3 at a full 20.0/20, against **81.0** and 6.0/20 from its reviews counted once; Brasitas 84.3 against 79.5. Always upward — the direction that flatters a prospect. Two further findings came from the same walk. Review retrieval had **no cache at all**, though discovery has had one since DEC-077 and reviews are the expensive half: the store holds 46 retained review pages of which only 23 are distinct, so **23 SerpApi credits — 9% of a monthly free tier (DEC-032) — were spent re-retrieving reviews already on disk**, in direct contradiction of DEC-020. And `CandidateScoreAction` received nothing from the restore, so a scored candidate reappeared as a blank row above a button reading "spends further SerpApi credits" — which is what caused the re-retrieval in the first place.
- Options considered: deduplicate the union of every retained page (rejected: it produces a set of reviews with no single honest `retrievedAt` to measure recency against, and a `paginationExhausted` flag belonging to none of the runs, while charter 9.7 computes every time-based factor against the stored retrieval timestamp of the evidence being measured); replay the most recent retrieval run per listing (chosen — a run is a coherent sample taken at one moment, and replaying it reproduces exactly what that retrieval saw); keep restore-side scoring and fix it there (rejected — see below).
- Decision: `electron/review-evidence.ts` (new, pure) splits a listing's retained pages into retrieval runs — a page requested without a `next_page_token` starts a run, read from the stored request rather than inferred from timing — and returns the latest. The same function serves both `session:restore` and a **new review-history cache**, so a restored session and a cache hit cannot disagree about what the evidence says. Retrieving again is still available as its own control, still stating its cost. **Scoring was removed from the restore entirely:** DEC-107 recomputed scores in `OperatorWorkspace` while `CandidateScoreAction` did the same thing from the live path, and the two drifted — that duplication *is* the defect. Restored evidence is now handed to the one component that scores, which shows the candidate with its reviews, its recorded judgment and its score, exactly as a fresh retrieval does.
- Consequences: after a restart the application tells the truth about what it knows. Confirmed live: Puerto Vallarta now reads 81.0/100 and Brasitas 79.5/100 in the running app, matching an independent recomputation from the store to the decimal, both `qualified` with all six gates passed. **This changes no scoring parameter**, so DEC-007/DEC-011's versioning rule is not engaged — the model was given inflated inputs, not different arithmetic; the same argument as DEC-097 and DEC-104. Any figure recorded from a *restored* session between DEC-107 and now is too high and should be reread from the application rather than trusted. The Shortlist's "not yet rankable" list now carries the reputation score and gate status it already had in hand, because a candidate at 84.3 with every gate passed, held up only by a measurement costing one PageSpeed request, had been reading the same as one nobody had looked at. Two smaller corrections: restoring a session no longer ticks the credit-spend acknowledgement — that checkbox is the operator's consent, and a restore is not the operator giving it, yet it left the application launched with the search button armed; and the SerpApi key is now read only when a request is actually going to be made, so a cache hit cannot fail for want of a credential it will not use. 17 new tests bring the suite to 438; four mutations were each confirmed to fail the new checks before they were trusted — using every run instead of the latest, dropping the duplicate guard, treating every page as a first page, and ignoring `forceRefresh` so the cache would have silently overridden an explicit request to spend. **Not addressed:** the trailing-window status still reads `complete_data` when SerpApi reports no further pages, which for a 4,781-review listing means the trailing year is covered by 8 retrieved reviews — accurate to what that flag means, and misleading as a word. Web-opportunity audits and the selected prospect are still not restored (DEC-107's own open items).
- Supersedes: not applicable — this corrects DEC-107 and extends DEC-077's caching rule to review retrieval.

### DEC-109 — `web-opportunity-v2`'s largest factor had never once been measured

- Date: 2026-08-09
- Status: **accepted, operator-directed, 2026-08-09.** Found on the first real PageSpeed measurement taken through the interface, while walking the flow to a demonstration preview.
- Context: DEC-097 reported mobile responsiveness — **30 of 100 points, the largest factor in the model that *orders* the shortlist** — as measured for the first time, reading `viewport`, `content-width` and `tap-targets` from the Lighthouse run each PageSpeed call already returns. Pointed at a real site it returned `unmeasured`, for two independent reasons, either of which alone was fatal. **First, the request never asked for them.** `buildPageSpeedMobilePlan` sent no `category` parameter, so the API returned the performance category alone — 47 audits, none of the three. **Second, the three audits no longer exist.** The API now serves Lighthouse 13.4.1, where `viewport` became `viewport-insight`, `tap-targets` became `target-size`, and `content-width` was removed outright. DEC-097's premise — "every PageSpeed call returns a full mobile Lighthouse run and only `audits.interactive` had ever been read from it" — was false as to the response, and its 19 tests all passed because every one of them fed a synthetic payload that contained the audits. The factor was `unmeasured` in exactly the way DEC-097 claimed to have fixed, and charter 10.4 was doing its job perfectly: the model reported an honest lower bound and never scored the absence as a failure, which is why nothing looked wrong.
- Options considered: read the successor audits only (rejected: the retained calibration evidence was captured under the old names and would stop reading); pin a Lighthouse version (rejected: the API serves what it serves, and this is not a dependency HORUS controls); name each slot across versions, newest first (chosen).
- Decision: the request asks for `performance`, `seo`, `accessibility` and `best-practices` by name, repeated as separate parameters — the API silently ignores a comma-joined value — which is still **one request and one quota unit**. `MOBILE_AUDIT_SLOTS` replaces the flat id list: each slot is an ordered list of names tried newest-first, so a response from either era reads. `meta-viewport` joins as a supporting signal, since a page that disables pinch-zoom is a real mobile defect and the old audit set had no check for it.
- Consequences: verified live against Brasitas — `viewport-insight`, `target-size` and `meta-viewport` all present in a 153-audit response, and the factor now reads **`0.0/30 (measured)`**: the site is genuinely fine on a phone, so there is no opportunity there. That is the distinction DEC-083's rule 4 and charter 10.4 exist for, and it is the first time this factor has produced a real value. **This changes no scoring parameter** — no weight, threshold or curve moves — so DEC-007/DEC-011 are not engaged; the same argument as DEC-097 and DEC-104. It does mean **every web-opportunity figure recorded before today was computed with 30 of 100 points absent**, including DEC-092's validation run, and shortlist ordering to date was driven by the factors that happened to work. 7 new tests bring the suite to 447; both mutations were confirmed to fail first — restoring the old id list, and dropping the legacy fallbacks. One new test asserts the main process's audit slice contains every id the slots may read, because that mismatch would send the factor `unmeasured` for a reason invisible in either file alone. **Named uncertainty for the operator:** `content-width` has no successor and nothing replaces it, so one of the three signals DEC-097 designed around is simply gone; and whether `viewport-insight`'s numeric score, thresholded at 0.9 like a binary audit, means what the old pass/fail `viewport` meant is a judgment this decision makes without evidence.
- Supersedes: not applicable — this corrects DEC-097's factual premise; its mapping and its charter 10.4 handling stand.

### DEC-110 — Bulk pre-screen and a distinct "awaiting judgment" shortlist reason

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Written by Claude at the operator's direction after two reported problems using the real application: the Search view showed every raw candidate with no automatic filtering, reading as "no curation at all"; and a generated demonstration preview showed mostly bracketed placeholders for one real business.
- Context: reaching a ranked candidate on the Shortlist view required pressing "Fetch review history & score" and "Measure web opportunity" on each of up to 20 raw candidates by hand, one at a time, before any ordering appeared — nothing pre-filtered or prioritized them. Separately, `ShortlistView`'s `not_reputation_qualified` exclusion reason ("scored, and did not reach the qualification threshold") was inaccurate for a real and common state: a candidate whose objective factors are fully measured but whose G4/G5/G6 judgment gates (charter 9.5) are still unanswered also reads `qualified: false` (`reputation-scoring.ts`'s `qualified` requires every gate to have `passed`), so a strong candidate awaiting only the operator's own reading of its reviews was indistinguishable on screen from one that had already been judged and fell short. The demonstration-preview report was investigated separately and found likely not to be a defect: `demonstration.ts` (DEC-106) already builds services/hours/photo sections from the listing's own Google data when present, verified live against Brasitas; a business without that data on its own Google listing will legitimately render placeholders rather than invent content (DEC-005), which a single report cannot distinguish from a bug without knowing whether the business's own name/address also failed to render.
- Options considered: have an LLM agent select and rank candidates directly (rejected for now — DEC-045 keeps scoring, ranking and qualification deterministic and outside model authority, and DEC-099/SECURITY_REVIEW.md finding F4 is still open, so routing the agent into the selection path reopens a documented, unresolved prompt-injection exfiltration risk without first closing it); leave the manual per-candidate flow as the only path (rejected — does not address the reported confusion); automate the existing deterministic per-candidate actions in bulk, behind one consent, and fix the shortlist labelling to separate "never scored," "scored, awaiting judgment," and "scored, judged, below threshold" (chosen).
- Decision: extracted the reputation-scoring and web-opportunity-audit construction logic that `CandidateScoreAction`/`CandidateWebOpportunityAction` had inline into a shared `src/views/candidate-scoring.ts` (`scoreCandidateFromHistory`, `auditCandidateFromMeasurement`, `isJudgmentPending`) so the existing manual path and a new automated path cannot compute a score differently — the same class of defect DEC-108 found and fixed for session restore vs. live scoring. `OperatorWorkspace` gained "Auto-screen candidates & rank": behind one consent checkbox stating the cost, it runs the free G1/G2 screen on every raw candidate, then sequentially retrieves review history and measures web opportunity (reusing DEC-077's discovery cache and DEC-108's review-history cache, so a repeat run spends nothing on evidence already retained) only for candidates that pass. `ShortlistCandidateInput` gained an optional `judgmentPending` field; `buildShortlist` reports a scored-but-unjudged candidate as the new `reputation_awaiting_judgment` reason instead of `not_reputation_qualified`, with every existing caller unaffected since the field defaults to falsy. `ShortlistView` now sorts the "not yet rankable" list by reputation lower bound, best first, display-only — `buildShortlist`'s own output order is untouched, so no existing domain test needed to change. **This produces a provisional score only** — G4/G5/G6 remain hardcoded to `insufficient_data` by the shared module's default judgment, so a bulk-prescreened candidate can never read `qualified: true` without the operator separately answering its judgment gates (DEC-008, hard rule 5); auto-screening is a worklist prioritizer, not a qualification shortcut.
- Consequences: the operator's stated confusion ("me muestra todas las opciones") is addressed for the free, objective part of the funnel — after one consent, the "not yet rankable" list becomes an ordered worklist rather than an unordered dump, and a strong, merely-unjudged candidate now reads distinctly from a genuinely weak one. Reading the reviews and answering G4-G6 remains manual by design and cannot be automated without violating DEC-008. The demonstration-preview report was not treated as a confirmed defect in this decision — it needs one more fact from the operator (did the business's own name/address also fail to render) before any change to `demonstration.ts` is warranted, and none was made. Verification could not be run in the environment that authored this change: `npm run lint`, `npm test` and `npm run build` need to be run on the operator's own macOS machine, the same limitation DEC-068 recorded for its own isolated-sandbox verification. Wiring the existing "Agent analyst" into candidate selection, as the operator separately asked about, was deliberately deferred out of this decision pending either closing SECURITY_REVIEW.md's finding F4 or an explicit operator decision to accept that risk.
- Supersedes: not applicable — extends DEC-075/DEC-103's shortlist design and DEC-071/DEC-072's per-candidate scoring actions without changing either's behavior for an existing caller.

### DEC-111 — A same-origin link check measures `broken_elements` for the first time

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Written by Claude at the operator's direction: "si el diseño está mal hecho o mal adaptado, si hay páginas que no cargan, quiero que un agente se encargue de eso... captura y que navegue por el sitio por encima. nada profundo, para detectar un mal website es suficiente por la superficie."
- Context: `web-opportunity-v2`'s `broken_elements` factor (18 of 100 points, `scoreBrokenElements` in `web-opportunity-audit.ts`) has read `unmeasured` for every candidate ever scored. DEC-072 fetched exactly one thing from a candidate's homepage — a `tel:` link check — and explicitly declined "a full link crawl," reasoning that broken-link detection needs either rendering a page or judgment a regex cannot honestly claim. That reasoning does not hold for the specific claim `checkedLinks`/`brokenLinks` needs: whether a same-origin link the page itself advertises returns an HTTP error is an objective, mechanical fact, not a judgment call — `scoreBrokenElements` has been ready to score a real `BrokenElementObservation` since DEC-072 was written; only the measurement was ever missing. Separately, the operator asked for an agent to judge design quality/adaptation and to navigate the site "por encima" to find broken pages, after the reputation judgment gates (G4-G6) conversation clarified that request was about web-opportunity, not about the operator-judgment gates charter 9.5 governs (those are untouched by this decision).
- Options considered: have an LLM agent navigate the site and judge both design and broken links together (rejected for now — conflates an objective, mechanical check with a genuinely subjective one, and full agent-driven navigation is exactly the shape `SECURITY_REVIEW.md` finding F4 warns about: a page or review could contain text engineered to steer an agent's next fetch off-site); do nothing and leave the factor unmeasured (rejected — a real, low-risk implementation was available and the operator asked for exactly this capability); implement the mechanical half deterministically, same-origin only, no agent involved, and treat the design-quality half as a separate, later decision (chosen).
- Decision: `electron/link-crawl.ts` (new) extracts same-origin `https:` links from the homepage HTML `runWebOpportunityMeasurement` already fetches for the `tel:` check (no extra request for the homepage itself), and checks up to 5 of them through the same `inspectPublicWebsiteReadOnly` every other network-reaching check in this codebase already uses (https-only, hostname-denylisted, timeouted, size-capped). A same-origin `http:` link, or a link to a different host, is skipped rather than counted broken — that would penalize a business for HORUS's own https-only tooling limit, not for anything wrong with its site. A link whose path contains "contact" sets `contactPath` to `verified-working` or `verified-broken` per `scoreBrokenElements`'s existing contract; a page with no checkable same-origin link returns `null`, read as `unmeasured` by the caller rather than as zero broken links. `WebOpportunityMeasurementResult` gained a `brokenLinks` field; `candidate-scoring.ts`'s `auditCandidateFromMeasurement` now feeds it into `broken_elements` instead of hardcoding `unmeasured`. **Deliberately excluded from this decision:** any judgment about visual design, branding, or "how outdated it looks" — that request is still open and needs its own decision, likely using the already-captured screenshot (DEC-078) and the existing, currently-idle Agent analyst, kept as a draft the operator reviews rather than a score, and probably after `SECURITY_REVIEW.md`'s finding F4 is addressed for that specific, narrower task.
- Consequences: `broken_elements` can now read `measured` on a real candidate for the first time, using no new paid API and no new SerpApi/PageSpeed credit — the added cost is a handful of plain HTTPS fetches per "Measure web opportunity" press (same control, same consent copy, no change needed there). Because a same-origin http link and an off-site link are both skipped rather than penalized, and a page with zero checkable links reads `unmeasured`, `scoreLowerBound` remains a genuine lower bound, never an inflated or deflated one from a false positive. **Every web-opportunity figure recorded before today was computed without this factor**, the same category of statement DEC-097/DEC-109 already made about mobile responsiveness — this changes no scoring parameter (DEC-007/DEC-011 not engaged), only what was previously absent. New tests: `tests/link-crawl.test.ts` (the crawl in isolation — same-origin filtering, the 5-link cap, http/off-site links skipped not penalized, network failures counted as broken, contact-path detection) and additions to `tests/web-opportunity-ipc.test.ts` and `tests/candidate-scoring.test.ts` (end-to-end wiring, and the `null`-means-unmeasured branch). **Not verified in this environment** — same limitation as DEC-110: `npm run lint`, `npm test` and `npm run build` need to run on the operator's own macOS machine.
- Supersedes: not applicable — completes the measurement DEC-072 explicitly left out, without changing `web-opportunity-audit.ts`'s existing, already-tested `scoreBrokenElements` scoring logic.

### DEC-112 — Shortlist redesigned as clickable cards, with a session-only Remove filter

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Written after the operator reported the Shortlist and Search views as "todo ese hijueputa texto pa que? si no lo puedo hacer click ni nada" — a wall of dense, one-line-per-candidate plain text with no clickable affordances — and asked for something simple and clean.
- Context: `ShortlistView` rendered `<ol>`/`<ul className="checklist">` plain-text lists, and the Search view's per-candidate line packed G1/G2 status, distance, and a "has a website field"/"within_5_miles" style sentence into one dense run of text with no way to act on a candidate the operator had already ruled out at a glance. Neither view offered a way to drop an obviously-irrelevant candidate from view without leaving it mixed in with the rest.
- Options considered: leave the text layout and only shorten the copy (rejected — the operator's complaint was about interactivity and density, not wording alone); redesign as clickable cards and add a display-only "Remove" filter (chosen).
- Decision: `ShortlistView`'s ranked and not-yet-rankable lists are now `.shortlist-card` components (rank/name, badge row, a short exclusion label with the full original sentence still visible underneath it per "evidence over scores," CLAUDE.md) instead of plain list items. The Search view's per-candidate line was shortened to name, rating, review count, G1/G2 status, and distance, dropping the verbose field-presence sentence. A new `dismissedIds: Set<string>` in `OperatorWorkspace` backs a "Remove" button per candidate and a "N removed — Restore all" line; it filters `visibleCandidates` for display only and never touches retained evidence (DEC-020's raw snapshots, charter 14, are untouched) — a fresh search or app restart clears it.
- Consequences: both views are clickable and scannable rather than dense text blocks. No scoring, gating, or evidence-retention logic changed. **Not verified in this environment** — `npm run lint`, `npm test` and `npm run build` need to run on the operator's own macOS machine.
- Supersedes: not applicable — extends DEC-075/DEC-103/DEC-110's shortlist design without changing `buildShortlist`'s own logic or output order.

### DEC-113 — Recording a qualifying judgment auto-selects the prospect and navigates there

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Written at the operator's explicit request: "que cuando una compania se registre y se guarde pase automaticamente al pantel de prospect y se quede guardado ahi hasta que haga alguna accion."
- Context: recording a judgment (G4/G5/G6) that made a candidate qualified previously left the operator on the same view; reaching the Prospect view for that candidate required a separate trip to the Shortlist to press "Select as prospect" on a candidate that had, in effect, just been selected by the operator's own judgment.
- Options considered: leave the two steps separate (rejected — the operator asked for this specific change); auto-select and navigate to Prospect the moment a recorded judgment makes a candidate qualified (chosen).
- Decision: `CandidateScoreAction`'s "Record this judgment" button gained an `onQualified?: (dataId: string) => void` prop, called only after the judgment is actually recorded and only when the just-recomputed score reads `qualified: true` — never on every keystroke or recompute. `OperatorWorkspace` wires both the Search view's per-candidate action and `ShortlistView`'s in-place expanded action to one handler, `onCandidateQualified`, which sets `selectedProspectId` and switches to the Prospect view — exactly what the existing "Select as prospect" button already did, triggered by the judgment-recording press instead of a second one.
- Consequences: this is navigation only. It does not publish a demonstration, contact a business, or cross either DEC-004 gate — both gates still require their own explicit operator action on the Demo review and Outreach views. **Not verified in this environment** — same standing limitation as DEC-110 through DEC-112.
- Supersedes: not applicable — reuses the existing "Select as prospect" state transition (DEC-102/DEC-075) unchanged; only what triggers it is new.

### DEC-114 — The demonstration generator adopts the project's own editorial visual language, plus two named references

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Written after the operator called a generated Roto-Rooter Plumbing demonstration "pauperrimo" and asked what happened to two repositories they had specified for website design — [pbakaus/impeccable](https://github.com/pbakaus/impeccable) and [Leonxlnx/taste-skill](https://github.com/Leonxlnx/taste-skill). Neither was recorded anywhere in this repository; `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s reference table read "To be defined... Pending" the whole time. Both are now recorded there, with what was actually adopted from each.
- Context: two separate gaps compounded. First, `apps/demo-template/` already held several hand-built concept sites from earlier in the project (SEASONS EATS, Finescape and Sons, Caribbean Bakery Mini Mart, Sunshine Cuisine) — real editorial layouts with a large serif headline, a colored decorative panel, an eyebrow-label system, and a dark "visit" card, built to charter section 15/DEC-025's real-data-only rule — but `buildDemonstrationSite`, the function the application actually calls, never adopted that visual language; it built a plain, generic template instead. A field-sparse listing (no services, hours, or price data on its own Google listing) left most of that plain template's sections empty, which is what read as "pauperrimo." Second, the two named repositories are both design-doctrine rulebooks for AI coding agents — prose rules and numeric tokens, not shippable CSS or components — so "what happened to them" is that they were never fetched or read at all until asked.
- Options considered: leave the generic template and treat the complaint as purely a data-sparsity problem (rejected — the CSS itself was also generic, independent of what data any given business has); copy `apps/demo-template/`'s hand-built HTML files directly per business (rejected — violates DEC-037's one-common-template rule, and does not scale to a candidate whose exact business type was never hand-built); rebuild `buildDemonstrationSite`'s template to match the hand-built sites' visual system, fold in the two named repositories' concrete, adoptable rules, and keep everything else (DEC-005 real-data-only, DEC-024's notice, the exact tested placeholder text) unchanged (chosen).
- Decision: `src/domain/demonstration.ts` keeps every existing field/placeholder/omission function unchanged (`field`, `telHref`, `servicesBlock`, `hoursBlock`, `photoBlock`, `priceBlock`, `reputationBlock`, `phoneBlock`, `websiteBlock` all produce byte-identical output to before) and only rebuilds the surrounding HTML/CSS: a hero section with a large serif headline (the business's own verified name), an eyebrow label (its own verified category), a colored decorative art panel behind the listing's own photo or a labelled placeholder, and a dark "Visit" card for address and current-website — all real data, no invented tagline or marketing copy. The accent color is derived deterministically from the business's own category string (`hueFromCategory`, a pure hash — same category, same hue, every run), not randomly, keeping `buildDemonstrationSite` a pure function (the existing "identical input produces byte-identical output" test). From `pbakaus/impeccable`: one accent color capped to a small page area, a warm cream background rather than pure white, fluid `clamp()` headline sizing, no shadow at rest with a shadow only on hover, and its written ban list (no colored side-borders, no gradient text, no pure black/white) used as a review checklist against the new CSS. From `Leonxlnx/taste-skill`: off-black rather than pure-black ink, tight letter-spacing on serif headings, and muted low-saturation tag-chip colors. Both repositories' actual deliverables — an npx CLI installer, an agent-skill runner, GSAP motion, Tailwind — were explicitly not adopted; neither fits a plain HTML string template with no build step. `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`'s reference table is filled in for the first time with both entries.
- Consequences: every demonstration `buildDemonstrationSite` produces from now on looks like the hand-built `apps/demo-template/` examples instead of the plain template — including for a data-sparse listing, where the visual system (not additional invented content) is what carries the page. No scoring, gating, or data-verification logic changed: this is presentation only. All 27 existing `tests/demonstration.test.ts` assertions (exact placeholder text, `<h2>Hours</h2>`, `[Business photography would appear here...]`, `alt="Photo published on..."`, the "Current website:" line's conditional presence, HTML-escaping, and the pure-function/byte-identical-output test) were checked by hand against the new template before this was written and are expected to still pass, since none of the functions they exercise changed — but per this repository's own standing limitation, `npm run lint`, `npm test` and `npm run build` still need to run on the operator's own macOS machine to confirm.
- Supersedes: not applicable — `buildDemonstrationSite`'s field logic (DEC-079, DEC-106, DEC-025) is unchanged; only its HTML/CSS assembly changes.

### DEC-115 — The Search view starts fresh on every launch; Shortlist and Prospect keep everything

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Written at the operator's request: "podrias hacer que en search cada vez q se abra se reinicie, solo en el panel de search. lo demas se debe quedar guardado para revisar mas tarde."
- Context: the request is genuinely ambiguous on two axes — whether "cada vez q se abra" means every application launch or every visit to the Search tab, and whether "se reinicie" means clearing the Search view's own display or also discarding the underlying discovery result that Shortlist and Prospect both depend on. **The reading chosen here, stated plainly rather than resolved silently (per this repository's own working convention):** "se abra" is read as application launch — switching to the Search tab mid-session does not clear anything, only closing and reopening HORUS does — and "se reinicie" is read as the Search view's own display only, never the underlying evidence. `OperatorWorkspace`'s session-restore effect already rebuilds `result`/`completed` from retained snapshots at launch (DEC-107/DEC-108) specifically so Shortlist and Prospect survive a restart; discarding that on restore would break "lo demas se debe quedar guardado," which the operator's own sentence explicitly asks to keep.
- Options considered: discard the restored discovery result entirely so Search truly starts from nothing (rejected — this is exactly what would break Shortlist and Prospect, which the operator explicitly asked to keep working); reset only on every tab switch to Search (rejected — re-reads "cada vez q se abra" as the tab, not the app, and would erase a search the operator is actively reviewing every time they navigate away and back, which is not what "se abra" ordinarily means for a desktop application); reset the Search view's own display at application launch only, leaving the restored evidence intact underneath for every other view (chosen).
- Decision: the session-restore effect in `OperatorWorkspace.tsx` no longer pre-fills the Search form's category/city inputs on restore (removed the `setInput(...)` call it previously made from the restored request). A new `searchResultsVisible` flag, `false` by default and after every restore, gates the Search view's "Candidates (...)" list and the "Auto-screen candidates" block; a fresh `run()` in the current session sets it `true` immediately, so a real search is always shown. When a restored result exists but has not been shown yet, the Search view instead shows one line naming the saved snapshot's candidate count and retrieval time, with a "Show it here too" button as an escape hatch, rather than either hiding the fact of a prior search entirely or showing it in full. `result`/`completed` and `retainedHistories` continue to be restored unconditionally, exactly as before — `ShortlistView` and `ProspectRecord` read from the same `completed`/`visibleCandidates` values regardless of `searchResultsVisible` and are unaffected by this decision.
- Consequences: opening the application now presents an empty Search form and no candidate list, rather than the last search's inputs and results, while a candidate already scored, judged, or selected as prospect in an earlier session remains exactly where it was on Shortlist and Prospect. No stored evidence, score, or judgment is discarded — this changes only what the Search view shows by default at launch. **Not verified in this environment** — `npm run lint`, `npm test` and `npm run build` need to run on the operator's own macOS machine; this was implemented after the last confirmed clean run (465/465 tests) that predates DEC-112 through DEC-115.
- Supersedes: not applicable — narrows DEC-107/DEC-108's unconditional session restore to the Search view's display only; the underlying restore behavior those decisions describe is unchanged.

### DEC-116 — DEC-114's demonstration template used CSS custom properties, which DEC-083 rule 6 forbids

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Found by the operator's own `npm test` run (464/465), and again by a second run after a first, incomplete fix (still 464/465) — both real test runs against DEC-114's rewrite, from the operator's own macOS machine.
- Context: DEC-083 rule 6 requires the demonstration generator to share no CSS tokens with the operator's own dark interface, enforced by `tests/contrast.test.ts`'s `'keeps the operator interface dark and the demonstration generator light'` assertion — which reads `src/domain/demonstration.ts`'s **own source text** via `readFileSync` (not the HTML it generates at runtime) and asserts that text never contains the literal substring `var(--`. DEC-114's rewrite introduced a `:root { --accent: ...; --ink: ...; }` block and used `var(--accent)` etc. throughout the generated stylesheet — a real regression this decision's own "not verified in this environment" caveat existed to catch, and did. The first fix pass replaced every CSS `var(--x)` with a literal but left the substring `var(--...)` alive inside an explanatory source comment describing the fix itself ("never a `:root`/`var(--...)` pair") — since the test greps the raw file text, that comment alone kept the assertion failing even with zero actual CSS custom properties left.
- Options considered: keep custom properties and change the test (rejected — DEC-083 rule 6's isolation reasoning still holds: a demonstration page must never be able to inherit or leak operator-theme tokens, and the test is what makes that isolation checkable); replace every `var(--x)` with a literal, computed once in JS and interpolated directly into the template string, and keep the source text itself free of the substring including in comments (chosen).
- Decision: `hueFromCategory`'s hue now feeds two JS string constants (`accent`, `accentSoft`) computed inline in `buildDemonstrationSite`, alongside four fixed literals (`ink`, `muted`, `line`, `paper`) that replace the old custom-property block. Every `var(--...)` reference in the generated `<style>` block was replaced with the matching literal, and the explanatory comment near `hueFromCategory` was reworded to describe the same thing without spelling out the forbidden substring. `:root { color-scheme: light; }` is kept (the test also asserts `color-scheme: light` is present) but no longer declares any custom property.
- Consequences: `buildDemonstrationSite` remains a pure function producing byte-identical output for identical input (nothing here introduces non-determinism), and both the generated page and the module's own source text are now free of `var(--`, restoring DEC-083 rule 6's isolation guarantee as the test actually checks it. **Not independently re-verified in this environment** — the operator's next `npm test` run is what confirms 465/465; this decision was written blind to that outcome once, already, and is stated cautiously here for that reason.
- Supersedes: not applicable — corrects an implementation defect in DEC-114; DEC-114's own design decisions (visual language, two named references, pure-function property) are otherwise unchanged.

### DEC-117 — Web-opportunity measurements are cached and restored, closing the credit-waste gap CLAUDE.md already named

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Written at the operator's explicit request: "quiero que cuando se haga una busqueda y se llenen los campos de las preguntas quede guardado en la memoria, me estas gastando los malditos creditos cada vez q uso la app."
- Context: unlike discovery search (DEC-077's cache) and review-history retrieval (DEC-108's cache), `discovery:measure-web-opportunity` had no caching at all — every press of "Measure web opportunity" spent a fresh PageSpeed quota unit, even for a URL just measured seconds earlier, and nothing about a prior measurement was restored on relaunch. This is exactly the gap `CLAUDE.md`'s own known-weaknesses list already named: "Web-opportunity audits and the selected prospect are still not restored when the application reopens (DEC-107, DEC-108)." Compounding it, `OperatorWorkspace`'s `scores`/`audits` state was only ever populated lazily, the moment a specific candidate's action component happened to mount — and DEC-115, built earlier this same session, made that worse by hiding the Search view's candidate list by default after a restore, removing the one place a `CandidateScoreAction` was reliably mounted. Together, a closed and reopened application showed a previously-ranked Shortlist as if nothing had ever been scored, and re-measuring a website cost a real credit every time.
- Options considered: leave web-opportunity unmeasured on restore and only fix reputation-score eagerness (rejected — PageSpeed is the more expensive, quota-limited credential of the two, and is exactly what the operator named); store a fully separate consolidated "measurement result" snapshot per run (rejected — a third raw write per measurement, and DEC-117 could reuse the two snapshots `runWebOpportunityMeasurement` was already storing); reconstruct a full measurement from the two snapshots (`pagespeed.mobile`, `horus.website-analysis`) already being stored, keyed by the target URL, and reuse that same reconstruction for both a same-session cache hit and a cross-session restore (chosen).
- Decision: both raw snapshots `runWebOpportunityMeasurement` already stores now carry a `targetUrl` field alongside their existing request data (mirroring DEC-077's `category`/`city` alongside `requestUrl`). The link-crawl result, previously computed but never persisted, is now stored inside the `horus.website-analysis` payload alongside the page text it was computed from — no third snapshot, no new request. A new `reconstructMeasurementFromSnapshots(url, pagespeedSnapshots, analysisSnapshots)` in `web-opportunity-ipc.ts` rebuilds a complete `WebOpportunityMeasurementResult` from those two snapshot types for one URL, re-deriving `servesHttps` from the URL itself (free, cannot go stale) rather than storing it. Two call sites use it and must agree: `runWebOpportunityMeasurement` checks it first (via an injected `findCachedSnapshots`, skipped when `forceRefresh` is set) before spending a PageSpeed unit, and `session:restore` calls it for every URL with retained evidence, returning `webOpportunityMeasurements` keyed by URL alongside the existing `reviewHistories`. `CandidateWebOpportunityAction` gained a `retained` prop and a mount-time restore effect, the same shape `CandidateScoreAction`'s DEC-108 restore effect already follows, plus a `fromCache`-aware "served from evidence, no credit spent" message and a `forceRefresh`-driven "Measure again" button distinct from the free "Re-check from retained measurement" default. `OperatorWorkspace`'s own restore effect now also computes `scores` and `audits` **eagerly, for every candidate with retained evidence**, rather than waiting for that candidate's own action component to mount — closing the compounding gap DEC-115 introduced.
- Consequences: a URL already measured, in this session or a prior one, is never billed again unless the operator explicitly presses "Measure again." Reopening the application now shows a previously-ranked Shortlist immediately, without the operator needing to expand every "not yet rankable" card by hand first. No scoring parameter changed (DEC-007/DEC-011 not engaged) — `auditCandidateFromMeasurement` and `web-opportunity-v2` are untouched; only what evidence reaches them, and when, changed. `runBulkPrescreen`'s own `measureWebOpportunity` call benefits automatically, since it already omits `forceRefresh` and so is cache-first by default. **Verified 2026-08-11** — the operator's own `npm run lint && npm test && npm run build` run (0 lint warnings/errors, 465/465 tests, clean build) confirmed this change alongside DEC-118 through DEC-121, all from this same session. Still **awaiting operator review** for acceptance.
- Supersedes: not applicable — extends DEC-072/DEC-097/DEC-098/DEC-111's measurement pipeline and DEC-107/DEC-108's restore model without changing either's own scoring or extraction logic.

### DEC-118 — The Tracker loads automatically on first view, instead of only on an explicit Refresh

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Written after the operator sent a real outreach email and reported the Tracker view as empty — "envie el correo y no me sale nada aqui" — then, asked, confirmed it appeared the moment they pressed "Refresh." The event had been recorded correctly the whole time; only the view had not fetched it yet.
- Context: `RealTrackerPanel` fetches `tracker:listEvents` only from two places — an explicit "Refresh" press, or a "Open tracker" button shown when the panel is collapsed. Its `open` state defaults to `true` and nothing in the component ever sets it to `false`, so the collapsed branch is unreachable in practice — meaning the panel is never actually collapsed-by-default the way `AnalystPanel`'s and the Search view's own auto-screen block are, the precedent DEC-082's original comment cited for fetching on demand. The practical result: arriving at Tracker right after a real, consequential action (a publish, an outreach open, a declared send) shows nothing until the operator remembers a button exists to ask for it — reading as "it didn't work," when it did.
- Options considered: leave it fetch-on-demand only and better-label the Refresh button (rejected — the operator's own report was about the view reading as broken at the moment it mattered most, right after a real send; a label does not fix a false negative); make the Tracker a live subscription (rejected — no push/event-stream mechanism exists between the main process and renderer for this, and building one is a disproportionate fix for a one-line problem); load once automatically when the view mounts, keeping "Refresh" for anything recorded after arriving (chosen).
- Decision: `RealTrackerPanel` gained a `useEffect` that calls the existing `load()` once on mount. Nothing else about the component changed — `entries` still starts `null`, the "no prospect... yet" message still only shows once a real (possibly empty) response has come back, and "Refresh" still exists and behaves exactly as before for an event recorded while already on this view (for example, pressing "Record a response" already calls `load()` the same way).
- Consequences: arriving at Tracker now reflects the true state of the durable event log immediately, without an extra press the operator has no reason to know is required. No new IPC surface, no change to what counts as a recorded event, no change to `buildTrackerView`, `tracker.ts`, or DEC-096's 60-day review logic. **Verified 2026-08-11** — the operator's own `npm run lint && npm test && npm run build` run (0 lint warnings/errors, 465/465 tests, clean build) confirmed this change alongside DEC-117 and DEC-119 through DEC-121. No existing test in this repository mounts `RealTrackerPanel` directly, so this specific change still carries no dedicated test coverage of its own, even though the full-suite run is clean. Still **awaiting operator review** for acceptance.
- Supersedes: not applicable — DEC-082's tracker design (a read-only projection over the append-only log, refetched rather than kept as a second stored copy) is unchanged; only when the first fetch happens is new.

### DEC-119 — A depth-and-motion polish pass on the operator interface, built entirely from existing DEC-083 tokens

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-10, awaiting operator review.** Written at the operator's explicit request: "podrias mejorar la interfaz en general? quiero que al menos se vea wow." A first version of this decision (shadows only) was shipped, and the operator immediately reported it as invisible: "que le hiciste al diseno? se ve igual!!" — correct, and addressed in a second pass on the same decision, described below rather than in a new one, since nothing had yet been marked accepted.
- Context: "quiero que se vea wow" is a real request but not a specific one, and DEC-083/DEC-036 already bind this interface to a deliberate, tested register — a dark, chroma-neutral instrument panel with exactly four semantic accent meanings, no filled gauges or animated values (rule 4), and the two DEC-004 approval gates carrying gravity with no transition, celebration, or success animation (rule 5), all enforced in part by `tests/contrast.test.ts` reading real hex values out of `src/index.css`. "Wow" and "still passes the existing design review" are only compatible if the change is depth and craft on the existing palette, not a new one — so the read chosen here, stated plainly per this repository's working convention, is: add shadow, motion and structural polish using only values already declared or newly declared as pure elevation/motion primitives, touch zero hex values `contrast.test.ts` reads, add zero new `--accent-*` token, and leave `.gate-zone` exactly as flat and static as DEC-083 rule 5 requires.
- Options considered: introduce a second, brighter accent or a livelier palette for visual energy (rejected — a fifth colour or a changed accent value is precisely what DEC-083 rule 1 and `tests/contrast.test.ts`'s exact-four-accents assertion forbid, and re-deriving new colours risks failing the 4.5:1/3:1 ratios the same test enforces without a very deliberate, separately-reviewed pass); add motion and celebratory feedback broadly, including on the two approval gates, to make pressing them feel rewarding (rejected — this is the literal case DEC-083 rule 5 exists to prevent: DEC-004's gates are supposed to feel consequential, not satisfying); add a layered shadow system and short (<=200ms) hover/motion polish built entirely from `index.css`'s existing surfaces and the new elevation primitives, applied to panels, cards and controls generally, with `.gate-zone` and its buttons explicitly exempted from anything hover- or transition-based (chosen).
- Decision: `App.css` gained one new `:root` block declaring elevation/motion primitives — `--shadow-sm`, `--shadow-md`, `--shadow-lg` (layered `rgba(0,0,0,...)` shadows), `--motion-lift` (160ms, `index.css`'s existing `--ease` curve), and, added in the second pass, `--edge-highlight`/`--edge-highlight-strong` (`rgba(255,255,255,...)` at low alpha) — added in `App.css`, not `index.css`, so every value `tests/contrast.test.ts` reads via `readHexTokens` is untouched by construction. The first pass applied only the dark shadows across `.stage-panel`, `.discovery-panel`/`.analyst-panel`, `.prospect-card`/`.preview-box`/`.message-preview`, `.empty-state`, `.review-evidence`, `.score-breakdown`, `.shortlist-card`, buttons, and `.workspace-header`/`.workspace-nav-item` — and, against `--surface-0` at `#0e1116`, a black shadow is nearly invisible, which is exactly what the operator reported.
