- Options considered: (1) keep investigating other theories (schema size, instruction length, `--max-turns`) — deprioritized once the type-array difference was found, since it is the one concrete, falsifiable structural difference between a task that hangs and three that don't; (2) drop the nullable fields entirely (require `tagline`/`aboutParagraph` to always be a string, using an empty string as "absent") — rejected, this would blur "the composer chose not to include this" with "the composer wrote nothing," which `parseComposerOutput`'s section-consistency checks (rule 4) depend on distinguishing; (3) rewrite the two fields' schema using `anyOf: [{ type: 'string' }, { type: 'null' }]` instead of a type array — the more conservative, universally-supported JSON Schema form for the same "string or null" contract — **chosen**, no other code changes needed since `parseComposerOutput` already checks the JS value (`raw.tagline !== null`), not the schema's shape.
- Decision: `concept-composer-task.ts`'s `COMPOSER_OUTPUT_SCHEMA` now declares `tagline: { anyOf: [{ type: 'string' }, { type: 'null' }] }` and `aboutParagraph: { anyOf: [{ type: 'string' }, { type: 'null' }] }` in place of the two `type: ['string', 'null']` declarations. Nothing else in the task, the parser, or the IPC wiring changed.
- Consequences: this is a **suspected, not confirmed** root cause — reasoned from the strongest evidence available without being able to run the operator's own Claude Code CLI directly, but not yet verified against a live run. If correct, "Compose with agent" should now complete rather than hang; if the operator reports another timeout after this change, DEC-132's captured-output detail becomes the next real lead, since a type-array-caused hang would most likely show no output at all (the model retrying against an unsupported schema construct, never getting anywhere it would write to stdout/stderr), which is itself now a distinguishing signal. **Not verified in this environment** — `npm run build:electron` and a live "Compose with agent" run are the next required steps, on the operator's own machine.
- Supersedes: not applicable — narrows the one schema declaration implicated; DEC-129's composer design and DEC-132's diagnostic improvement are otherwise unchanged.

### DEC-134 — The evidence view collapses to one yes/no decision per business; the two DEC-004 gates stay separate

- Date: 2026-08-11
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-11, awaiting operator review.** Written after the operator asked directly for a more minimalist interface: "sin tantos clicks. cuando eligo un negocio que sea un solo boton de si o no quiero seguir." Asked how far to take it — through qualification and demo generation only, or all the way through publishing and sending — the operator chose the narrower, recommended option: collapse everything up to generating a demo, keep the publish and outreach gates as their own explicit steps.
- Context: the evidence view for a selected prospect had accumulated, decision by decision (DEC-095's refresh button, DEC-078's screenshot capture, DEC-129's composer control, DEC-131's qualification buttons), into five separate manual controls the operator had to press in sequence to get from "I picked this business" to "here's a demo to review." Each addition was individually justified at the time; together they were the opposite of what the operator now wants.
- Options considered: (1) collapse the entire pipeline, including publish and outreach, into the same yes/no button — offered directly, and declined; keeping those two gates as separate, explicit approvals is DEC-004's own point (a real public site, a real email, neither undoable from HORUS the way a demo preview is) and folding them into a generic "continue" would mean an operator could publish or send without ever reading a checkbox that says so; (2) collapse qualification through demo generation into one button, and hide the underlying manual controls (refresh, screenshot, raw scores) behind a collapsed `<details>` to also declutter the view — the second half was cut from what shipped: it could not be verified against `tests/interface-wiring.test.tsx` and `tests/freshness-gates.test.tsx`'s existing assertions (`getByRole('button', { name: /refresh this business/i })` and similar) without running the operator's own test suite first, and jsdom's handling of a closed `<details>`'s accessibility tree was not confidently known well enough to risk the 533-test baseline on it; (3) collapse qualification/compose/generate into one button, leave the manual controls exactly where and how they already render — **chosen** — the safer version of (2), verified only by build and lint in this environment.
- Decision: `ProspectRecord.tsx` gained `runFullFlow`, triggered by a new "Sí, seguir" button at the top of the evidence view: calls `orchestrator:advance-qualification` (DEC-130/131); if the agent qualifies the lead (or it was already `QUALIFIED` from an earlier press this session — checked via `lead:get-state` rather than assumed from a `skipped` result), runs the composer (DEC-129) best-effort, then renders the demo preview with whatever composition it got, all without a further button press. A `REJECTED` or `FAILED` outcome stops there rather than generating a demo for a business the pipeline itself declined to pursue, with the reason shown in place. "No, siguiente" is the existing `onClear` handler under a clearer label, next to "Sí, seguir" rather than at the bottom of the view. `generateDemoPreview` was split into a new `renderDemo(composition)` taking an explicit argument, because calling `setComposerResult` and then reading `composerResult` in the same synchronous function would see the pre-update value — React state updates are not applied mid-function — which would have silently rendered the deterministic fallback even right after the composer succeeded. The standalone "Run qualification"/"Check status" controls (DEC-131) remain, relabeled "Run qualification only", for when the operator wants to re-qualify without also regenerating the demo; every other manual control (refresh, screenshot, raw score/audit display) is unchanged and unmoved.
- Consequences: choosing a business is now one button in the common case — qualify, compose, and preview happen together — while an operator who wants to inspect raw evidence, force-refresh, or re-qualify independently can still do exactly what they could before. The view is not visually shorter (option (2)'s declined half), so "less clutter" is only partly delivered; a follow-up pass that verifies `<details>` collapsing against the real test suite (rather than reasoning about jsdom's accessibility tree from outside it) could close that gap safely, once the operator can run it. **Not verified in this environment** — `npm run lint && npm test && npm run build`, and clicking through "Sí, seguir" on a real prospect, still need to happen on the operator's own machine; existing tests were read carefully for conflicts (no test asserts the removed button labels, and the source-level freshness-gate assertions target code this decision did not touch) but were not run.
- Supersedes: not applicable — reorders and adds to the evidence view; DEC-004's two gates, DEC-078/DEC-095/DEC-129/DEC-130/DEC-131's underlying mechanisms are all unchanged.

### DEC-136 — The three judgment gates (G4/G5/G6) are asked as one question in the interface; the domain still records three answers

- Date: 2026-08-11
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-11, awaiting operator review.** Written after the operator saw the G4/G5/G6 controls (three dropdowns, each defaulting to "Not assessed yet") and said: "esas opciones no me interesan que aparezcan. pon todo en 1 sola opcion." Told directly that this is exactly what DEC-091 considered and rejected — a single checkbox "records no rationale," and the charter asks three distinct questions with different consequences — and given three concrete options with that tradeoff spelled out (compact three-button row keeping the per-gate record; a single button that marks all three at once but loses the reason for a "no"; or truly merging the three questions into one, discarding the distinction altogether), the operator chose the third: a real merge.
- Context: `CandidateActions.tsx`'s `CandidateScoreAction` rendered `JUDGMENT_GATES` (from `operator-judgment.ts`, DEC-091) as three separate `<select>` controls plus an optional rationale `<input>` each — G4 complaint pattern, G5 operational status, G6 listing identity. DEC-091 built these because, before it, all three were hardcoded to `insufficient_data` with no interface at all, which made qualification permanently unreachable. DEC-124 later removed the rationale requirement at the operator's own request but kept the three-question structure. This decision is the operator asking to go further than DEC-124 did.
- Options considered: (1) a compact row of three yes/no buttons in place of the three dropdowns and text inputs — fewer clicks, but still three questions and still preserves which gate failed when a candidate does not qualify — declined by the operator in favor of a real merge; (2) one button that answers all three fields at once ("yes" passes all three, "no" leaves them unanswered) — one click, but a "no" would carry no record of which of the three was the actual problem — also declined; (3) collapse the interface to one literal question, discarding the three-way distinction — **chosen**, the exact design DEC-091 evaluated and rejected at the time, now adopted at the operator's explicit, informed request.
- Decision: `operator-judgment.ts` (the domain module DEC-091 built, and DEC-124 amended) is untouched — `OperatorJudgmentDraft`'s three fields, `JUDGMENT_GATES`, `resolveJudgment`, and `buildReputationScore`'s three gates in `reputation-scoring.ts` all still exist and still behave exactly as their own tests describe. Only `CandidateActions.tsx` changes: it no longer renders `JUDGMENT_GATES` as three controls. A new single `<select>` ("Not assessed yet" / "Sí, checks out" / "No, it doesn't") writes to all three underlying fields through two small local functions, `draftFromSingleVerdict` and `singleVerdictFromDraft`. "Sí" sets all three verdicts to their passing value (`none_found`/`active`/`confirmed`) with a shared evidence string naming this as a DEC-136 merged answer. "No" sets only `complaintPattern` to `pattern_found` — sufficient on its own to keep the candidate unqualified, since `buildReputationScore` requires every gate to pass — and leaves `operationalStatus`/`listingIdentity` at `insufficient_data` rather than inventing a specific reason for fields the merged question never actually asked about. The completion hint below the control (previously "not all three gates are answered") now reads off the single merged state instead of `isJudgmentComplete`, since a deliberate "no" answer is now complete without touching all three fields individually.
- Consequences: the operator answers one question instead of three, and the visible UI can no longer say *which* of complaint pattern, operational status, or listing identity was the problem when a candidate doesn't qualify — that per-gate reasoning, which DEC-091 built this structure specifically to preserve, is lost at the interface layer for anything recorded through this control from now on. The underlying record still shows three gate statuses (visible in `score.gates` and in the judgment log), so a judgment recorded this way is not literally indistinguishable from one recorded before this change — but a "no" now always shows the same one gate (`complaintPattern`/G4) as the failing one regardless of the real reason, which is not a true account of what failed; the evidence string on that gate says plainly that the real reason wasn't captured, so nothing here claims to know more than it does. If a future review needs the specific reason back, `reputation-scoring.ts` and `operator-judgment.ts` were not touched and can support it without any domain-level change — only `CandidateActions.tsx` would need to change again.
- Supersedes: DEC-091's presentation decision only ("three separate questions, one per gate") — DEC-091's structural rule (three gates exist, the default is never a pass, `insufficient_data` blocks qualification) and DEC-124's rationale-optional rule are both unchanged and still enforced by `operator-judgment.ts`/`reputation-scoring.ts` exactly as before. **Not verified in this environment** — `npm run lint && npm test && npm run build`, and clicking through the new single control on a real candidate, still need to happen on the operator's own machine. No existing test asserts on the three-gate UI structure specifically (checked `tests/interface-wiring.test.tsx` and grepped the whole `tests/` directory for "JUDGMENT_GATES" and "judgment-gate" outside `CandidateActions.tsx` itself — no hits), so none is expected to break; `tests/operator-judgment.test.ts` covers the untouched domain module and was not affected by this change either way.

### DEC-137 — A manual, operator-only retry for a lead stuck at FAILED

- Date: 2026-08-11
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-11, awaiting operator review.** Written after a live "Sí, seguir" run (DEC-134) hit a real "Qualification agent failed: timeout" and left the business permanently unreachable through either "Sí, seguir" or "Run qualification only" — both call `advanceLeadQualification`, which only ever acts on a `DISCOVERED` lead, and `lead-state.ts` deliberately gives `FAILED` zero outgoing transitions (`tests/lead-state.test.ts`'s "refuses any transition out of a terminal state" checks this for every status, `FAILED` included). Told this plainly — that reversing it would touch a test written on purpose to keep `FAILED` closed — the operator chose to add a retry rather than leave it.
- Context: `FAILED` covers infra failures (a timed-out or crashed agent run) the same way it would cover something more fundamentally wrong — the state machine does not distinguish them. A business the qualification agent never actually got to evaluate, because the run itself broke, is not a business the pipeline decided against; leaving it permanently stuck reads as a judgment it never made.
- Options considered: (1) add `FAILED: ['QUALIFYING']` to `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` in `lead-state.ts` — rejected: this is a general edge, available to anything that calls `appendValidatedLeadStatus`, including some future automated caller, which is exactly what the existing terminal-state test (and the original design distinguishing `FAILED` from `QA_FAILED`'s real correction-loop edge) was written to prevent; it would also have required rewriting that test's own assertion rather than adding to it; (2) a narrow, separate, operator-only door — **chosen**.
- Decision: `lead-store.ts` gains `appendOperatorRetryFromFailed`, which checks `current.status === 'FAILED'` itself and writes `QUALIFYING` directly — it does not call `isValidLeadTransition`, so `lead-state.ts`'s `FAILED: []` and its terminal-state test are both completely untouched. `run-lead.ts`'s `advanceLeadQualification` was refactored to extract the part after a lead reaches `QUALIFYING` (run the agent, record `QUALIFIED`/`REJECTED`/`FAILED`) into a shared `runQualificationAndRecord`, now called by a new `retryLeadQualification`, which requires `current.status === 'FAILED'`, calls `appendOperatorRetryFromFailed`, then shares the exact same run-and-record path a first attempt uses. New IPC channel `orchestrator:retry-qualification` (`preload.cts`'s `orchestrator.retryQualification`), reachable only from a new "Reintentar calificación" button in `ProspectRecord.tsx`, shown only once `leadStatus.status === 'FAILED'`. Nothing in the automated pipeline calls either the new store function or the new IPC channel.
- Consequences: a lead that failed for an infra reason is no longer permanently unreachable through the agent pipeline — the operator can retry it as many times as they choose, each retry a full first attempt at qualification (fresh evidence assembly, fresh agent run). `isLeadTerminal('FAILED')` still reports `true` and `TERMINAL_STATUSES` is unchanged: automation still treats `FAILED` as a dead end and will never retry on its own, which is the distinction this decision preserves — only a human pressing the new button can move a lead out of `FAILED`. New tests: `tests/lead-store.test.ts` (`appendOperatorRetryFromFailed` — succeeds only from `FAILED`, refuses everything else including `QUALIFYING`) and `tests/run-lead.test.ts` (`retryLeadQualification` — success, repeat failure, skip when not `FAILED`). `tests/lead-state.test.ts` was read and confirmed unaffected — this decision adds no new case to its enumeration, it adds a second, separate write path outside `isValidLeadTransition` entirely. **Not verified in this environment.**
- Supersedes: not applicable — `lead-state.ts`'s transition table and terminal-state guarantee are unchanged; this adds one new, narrowly-scoped write path alongside `appendValidatedLeadStatus`, not a change to it.

### DEC-138 — `nodeSpawn` escalates to SIGKILL if a timed-out process does not exit

- Date: 2026-08-11
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-11, awaiting operator review.** Written from the same live timeout that prompted DEC-137: the failed run's own captured output (DEC-132) showed `"duration_ms":115467` — the child process had already produced a terminal result about 4.5 seconds before HORUS's 120-second timeout fired, meaning it (or an MCP server child of it) kept running past that point without ever emitting `close`.
- Context: `nodeSpawn` sent `SIGTERM` on timeout and then only waited — a process that does not act on `SIGTERM` (or an orphaned child of it still holding the stdio pipes open) would hold the whole run open indefinitely past its own logical end, with nothing to force it closed.
- Options considered: (1) leave it as `SIGTERM`-only — rejected, it is what produced the exact symptom being diagnosed; (2) send `SIGKILL` immediately instead of `SIGTERM` — rejected, `SIGTERM` gives a process a chance to clean up and remains the correct first signal; (3) `SIGTERM`, then `SIGKILL` after a grace period if the process has not exited — **chosen**, the standard pattern for this exact problem.
- Decision: `node-spawn.ts` adds `KILL_GRACE_MS` (5 seconds). On timeout, `nodeSpawn` still sends `SIGTERM` first; if the process has not settled within `KILL_GRACE_MS`, it sends `SIGKILL`, which a process cannot ignore. The grace timer is cleared in `finish()` alongside the existing timeout timer, so a process that does exit promptly after `SIGTERM` is unaffected.
- Consequences: a run that hangs after `SIGTERM` now ends close to its configured `timeoutMs` plus 5 seconds, instead of running until the OS or something else eventually reclaims it — HORUS's own reported "timeout" duration becomes closer to true. This is a robustness fix, not a fix for whatever caused the child to stop responding to `SIGTERM` in the first place, which remains unconfirmed (see DEC-139). `tests/node-spawn.test.ts`'s existing timeout test spawns a `node` process with no signal handler, which exits immediately on `SIGTERM` — read and confirmed it completes well inside the existing 200ms timeout the test uses, so this change does not affect it. **Not verified in this environment.**
- Supersedes: not applicable — additive to `nodeSpawn`'s existing timeout behavior.

### DEC-139 — `read_evidence_snapshot` caps how much text it returns per snapshot

- Date: 2026-08-11
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-11, suspected root cause, not confirmed.** The same live timeout's captured output (DEC-132) showed the qualification agent attempting a raw shell command — `tail -c 60000` against a file under a Claude Code `tool-results` directory — rather than reasoning directly over `read_evidence_snapshot`'s response. This is the shape Claude Code CLI is understood to take when a tool result is too large to return inline: it writes the result to a file and expects the model to inspect it another way. The operator was told this is a strong lead from real evidence, not a confirmed diagnosis, before asking for this fix.
- Context: `read_evidence_snapshot` (`evidence-mcp-server.ts`) returned `JSON.stringify(snapshot)` with no limit. A `serpapi.google_maps_reviews` snapshot for a business with many long reviews is exactly the shape that could grow large enough to trip whatever threshold triggers that file-offload behavior — and a bounded agent role's `allowedTools` never includes a shell, so if the CLI does hand it a file instead of inline text, there is no allowed way for the agent to read it, which would explain both the wasted turns and the eventual hang DEC-138 addresses.
- Options considered: (1) leave it unbounded and wait for a confirmed root cause before changing it — declined; the operator asked to fix it now, from what the evidence already shows; (2) truncate the returned text at a fixed length, clearly labelled as truncated — **chosen**; (3) restructure what `read_evidence_snapshot` returns per source type (e.g. cap review text specifically) — more precise, deferred as unnecessary complexity until (2) is confirmed insufficient.
- Decision: `evidence-mcp-server.ts` adds `MAX_SNAPSHOT_TEXT_CHARS` (20,000). A snapshot whose serialized JSON exceeds that length is truncated to the first 20,000 characters, with an appended, explicit note stating the original length, that the JSON is cut off mid-structure, and that the agent should not conclude something is absent just because it is missing from the excerpt — the same "large sample, not the complete record" framing DEC-127's evidence-scoping comments already use elsewhere in this file. Small snapshots (the common case, and everything any existing test constructs) are returned exactly as before.
- Consequences: an agent reasoning over a business with unusually long review history now sees a large, clearly-labelled excerpt instead of either the full payload (whatever was previously triggering the file-offload path) or nothing. This is explicitly not certain to be the actual cause of the timeout — if a live retry on the same business still fails the same way, this can be ruled out and DEC-132's captured detail should be read again for what the CLI reports next. No existing test reads `evidence-mcp-server.ts` directly (there is no `evidence-mcp-server.test.ts`; it runs as a separate spawned process, consistent with `analyst-ipc.test.ts`/`qualification-ipc.test.ts` testing the IPC layer around agent runs rather than the MCP server binary itself), so nothing needed updating. **Not verified in this environment**, and specifically not verified against the real failing case — the operator retrying "Auto Tech Imported Car Service" (or any lead that previously failed, via DEC-137's new "Reintentar calificación") is the actual test of whether this helped.
- Supersedes: not applicable — `read_evidence_snapshot`'s contract (source, retrieval timestamp, payload) is unchanged for any snapshot under the size cap; only an oversized snapshot's text now differs from before.

### DEC-140 — The two design references are installed and enforced rather than transcribed; the composer gains bounded design authority; the BUILD → QA → FIX loop is wired

- Date: 2026-08-11
- Status: **proposed 2026-08-11, awaiting operator review.** Written after the operator asked three things: whether the agents in the agent folder are actually used, which design skill the website generator was supposed to be using and why it is not, and to make the system robust enough to keep working on its own.
- Context: three separate findings, each verified against the code rather than inferred.
  1. **The agents.** Of the five roles declared in `runtime.ts`, `qa_reviewer` was complete and had its own passing test (`tests/qa-ipc.test.ts`) but was unreachable in production — nothing outside that test imported `qa-ipc.ts`, and it had no IPC channel, no preload binding and no renderer type. A tested module that no running code can call is still dead code. `outreach_composer` remains a type with no implementation (DEC-081, deliberate). The Orchestrator was one step long: `run-lead.ts` wired DISCOVERED → QUALIFIED and stopped, and its own header explained why — `buildDemonstrationSite` lived under `src/domain/`, which `tsconfig.electron.json` could not import.
  2. **The design references were never actually used.** DEC-114 recorded `pbakaus/impeccable` and `Leonxlnx/taste-skill` as "Adopted" in `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md`. Neither repository was ever installed and neither was ever run; roughly eight token-level rules were transcribed from their READMEs into CSS constants by hand. **Two came across inverted.** impeccable classifies a warm cream page background as `cream-palette`, an anti-pattern — "the default 'tasteful' AI surface, reached for by reflex" — and taste-skill bans the same warm-paper family outright as its "second-most-recurring AI-tell"; DEC-114 adopted it as a rule *from* impeccable. The same happened with the hero eyebrow label (`hero-eyebrow-chip`, and `kicker-above-heading`, which impeccable's craft floor calls "a ban, not a default: no brief earns it back"), added by DEC-114 and reinforced by DEC-123. Running impeccable's real detector against the shipped generator's real output for the first time returned **six blocking findings**: `cream-palette`, `hero-eyebrow-chip`, `tiny-text` ×2 and `cramped-padding` ×2.
  3. **Why a skill could not have been "enforced" as installed.** DEC-057 gives every Claude Code run a fresh empty working directory and `working-directory.ts` throws if a `.claude/` directory is present; `runtime.ts` additionally passes `--system-prompt`, replacing the default system prompt. A skill directory is therefore structurally unreachable from a HORUS agent run — installing one would have changed nothing.
- Options considered, per finding. For design authority the operator was asked directly and chose the middle of three: (a) keep the template and widen only content choices (rejected — leaves the "one template with the content swapped" complaint unaddressed); (b) **agent selects validated design tokens, HORUS owns all HTML structure (chosen)**; (c) agent authors free-form HTML/CSS with post-hoc validation (rejected by the operator's own choice — it reverses DEC-037 and puts DEC-024's mandatory notice and `noindex` behind a validator instead of behind construction). For enforcement: transcribe more rules by hand (rejected outright — transcription is exactly what failed here) versus **run the real detector as a gate (chosen)**. For the detector dependency: vendor a fork (rejected — goes stale) versus **depend on the published package (chosen)**; `--omit=optional` was tried to exclude puppeteer and reverted after it broke the toolchain (rolldown's own native binding is an optional dependency), so puppeteer is installed but its Chromium postinstall is explicitly refused in `allowScripts`, and only the static `detectHtml` engine is ever called.
- Decision:
  - `impeccable@3.5.0` (Apache-2.0) is a real dependency. `electron/qa/impeccable-gate.ts` runs its static `detectHtml` engine over each built demonstration. `error` and `warning` block; `advisory` does not, per impeccable's own severity vocabulary — there is deliberately no local exemption list, because a local exemption list is how DEC-114 drifted from its source. A detector that cannot run returns `unavailable`, never `passed` (charter 9.6/10.4).
  - `src/domain/demonstration.ts` moves to `shared/demonstration.ts`; `tsconfig.electron.json` becomes `rootDir: "."`, `outDir: "build"`, which leaves `build/electron/main.js` at its exact existing path. This resolves the boundary `run-lead.ts` and `lead-state.ts` both documented as blocking.
  - DEC-114's cream and its eyebrow are removed. Six curated palettes replace them, each one of taste-skill's own named "rotate, do not reuse" alternatives; three OS-available font pairings, all checked against impeccable's `OVERUSED_FONTS` and taste-skill's Fraunces/Instrument-Serif ban. The category label survives the eyebrow's deletion by moving into the top navigation — impeccable's own prescribed remedy. The `bold` tone's `accentStripe` (`border-left: 4px solid`, i.e. `side-tab`, "the most recognizable tell of AI-generated UIs", firing 144 times across the generated matrix) becomes an accent-coloured heading. The type scale is collapsed to clear the ≥2.0 hierarchy ratio, and the hero gains the horizontal padding it never had — `.hero`'s own `padding: 1.5rem 0 3rem` had been overriding `.wrap`'s, leaving hero content flush against the viewport edge on mobile, a real defect no one had reported.
  - `concept_composer` gains `palette` and `fontPairing`, both **enums over closed sets imported from `shared/demonstration.ts`**, so what the agent may choose and what the renderer can render cannot drift. `parseComposerOutput` rejects a value outside either set rather than falling back to a default — a silent fallback would make an agent's design choice unauditable. The agent still never writes a colour, a font, a line of CSS or a line of HTML. `electron/agent/design-doctrine.ts` carries both repositories' doctrine into the instruction, which is the only way to enforce it without weakening DEC-057.
  - `electron/orchestrator/run-demonstration.ts` wires QUALIFIED → WEBSITE_GENERATING → WEBSITE_GENERATED → QA_IN_PROGRESS → QA_PASSED/QA_FAILED, with a correction loop capped at `MAX_BUILD_ATTEMPTS = 3`. Deterministic checking runs before agent judgment (audit §I), so a page failing a mechanical rule never costs a QA agent run. Each attempt is retained as its own immutable `horus.demonstration_draft` snapshot and cited by the QA agent like any other evidence. On exhaustion the lead stops at QA_FAILED with its findings recorded — never forced through, never silently dropped, and never escalated to FAILED, because an uncorrectable demonstration is a judgment call, not an infrastructure error. `qa_reviewer` is reachable for the first time.
- Consequences: 673 tests pass, 0 lint warnings, clean build. **All 108 generated design combinations** (6 palettes × 3 fonts × 3 tones × field-complete/field-sparse) now return zero blocking and zero advisory findings from impeccable's own detector, down from 6 blocking findings on the shipped template; `tests/demonstration-design.test.ts` runs that whole matrix, so a regression fails the suite rather than shipping. `tests/impeccable-gate.test.ts` proves the gate can be made to fail, against a page built to trip six named rules. The DEC-004 gates are untouched: the loop's terminal state, QA_PASSED, means "ready for the operator to read", and `renderDemo`'s approval reset still applies. **Not verified against a live agent run** — every test drives a fake runtime, and whether a real model reliably picks a sensible palette per business is unmeasured. `apps/demo-template/`'s hand-built concept sites are still not what the code produces; this narrows that gap but does not close it.
- Supersedes: DEC-114's two inverted rules (the cream background and the hero eyebrow) are reversed outright, and its "Adopted" claim in `docs/DESIGN_REFERENCES.md` is replaced by an enforced one. DEC-122's `ACCENT_PALETTE` and DEC-123's eyebrow mark are superseded. DEC-129's composer schema is extended, not replaced. DEC-131's "website generation and QA remain unwired" no longer holds. DEC-037, DEC-024, DEC-005, DEC-025, DEC-045, DEC-057 and DEC-083 are all untouched and continue to bind in full.
